### Towards Resilient Safety-driven Unlearning for Diffusion Models against Downstream Fine-tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16302v1)
- **Authors**: Boheng Li, Renjie Gu, Junjie Wang, Leyi Qi, Yiming Li, Run Wang, Zhan Qin, Tianwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have achieved impressive image generation quality and are increasingly fine-tuned for personalized applications. However, these models often inherit unsafe behaviors from toxic pretraining data, raising growing safety concerns. While recent safety-driven unlearning methods have made promising progress in suppressing model toxicity, they are identified to be fragile to downstream fine-tuning, where we reveal that state-of-the-art methods largely fail to retain their effectiveness even when fine-tuned on entirely benign datasets. To mitigate this problem, in this paper, we propose ResAlign, a safety-driven unlearning framework with enhanced resilience against downstream fine-tuning. By modeling downstream fine-tuning as an implicit optimization problem with a Moreau Envelope-based reformulation, ResAlign enables efficient gradient estimation to minimize the recovery of harmful behaviors. Additionally, a meta-learning strategy is proposed to simulate a diverse distribution of fine-tuning scenarios to improve generalization. Extensive experiments across a wide range of datasets, fine-tuning methods, and configurations demonstrate that ResAlign consistently outperforms prior unlearning approaches in retaining safety after downstream fine-tuning while preserving benign generation capability well.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Towards Resilient Safety-driven Unlearning for Diffusion Models against Downstream Fine-tuning" addresses the vulnerability of safety-driven unlearning methods in text-to-image diffusion models to downstream fine-tuning.  The authors observe that existing unlearning techniques, while initially effective at reducing harmful content generation, often fail to maintain their effectiveness after fine-tuning, even when the fine-tuning data is benign. To counter this, they propose ResAlign, a framework that incorporates the anticipated effects of fine-tuning into the unlearning process. ResAlign models fine-tuning as an implicit optimization problem, enabling gradient estimation to minimize the recovery of harmful behaviors.  It also uses a meta-learning strategy to simulate diverse fine-tuning scenarios. The paper presents empirical results demonstrating that ResAlign outperforms existing unlearning approaches in maintaining safety after fine-tuning, while preserving the ability to generate both general and personalized images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Significant Finding on Existing Methods:** The paper's initial observation that existing unlearning methods are surprisingly fragile and easily reversed by fine-tuning on benign data is a significant and novel contribution. This highlights a practical vulnerability that was previously under-appreciated.
*   **ResAlign Framework:** The proposed ResAlign framework is novel in its approach to explicitly accounting for downstream fine-tuning during the unlearning process. Modeling fine-tuning as an implicit optimization problem using a Moreau Envelope is a clever technique that allows for efficient gradient estimation.
*   **Meta-Learning for Generalization:** The use of meta-learning to simulate a distribution of fine-tuning scenarios enhances the generalizability of the unlearning process, addressing a key limitation of previous methods that might overfit to specific fine-tuning configurations.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Real-World Problem:** The fragility of safety-driven unlearning methods is a critical issue in the deployment of diffusion models. The ability to fine-tune models on personalized data is becoming increasingly common, so unlearning needs to be robust to this. ResAlign addresses a real-world problem that has direct implications for the safety and responsible use of these models.
*   **Impact on Future Research:** By demonstrating the vulnerability of existing methods, the paper opens up new research avenues in the field of unlearning. It encourages the development of more resilient unlearning techniques that can withstand downstream adaptation. The use of Moreau Envelopes and meta-learning provides a solid foundation for future research.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation of ResAlign across a wide range of datasets, fine-tuning methods, and hyperparameters. This rigorous evaluation strengthens the credibility of the results and demonstrates the practical effectiveness of the proposed framework.
* **Limitations** The model is tested using limited model types and the generalization of the study could be increased by testing with additional models

**Strengths:**

*   Clear problem statement and motivation.
*   Well-defined ResAlign framework with a sound theoretical basis.
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation demonstrating the effectiveness of ResAlign.
*   Effective use of Moreau Envelopes and meta-learning techniques.
*   Provides a new perspective on the vulnerability of existing unlearning methods.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Overhead:**  Simulating fine-tuning introduces additional computational overhead, which, while manageable, could be a limitation in certain scenarios.
*   **Approximation:** The Moreau envelope approximation introduces some inaccuracies, although the experiments suggest that it is reasonably accurate.
*   **Dependence on Meta-Variables:** The meta-learning approach relies on defining a distribution of fine-tuning configurations, which may require some tuning and could potentially limit generalizability if the chosen distribution is not representative of real-world scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of safety-driven unlearning by identifying and addressing a key vulnerability in existing methods. The proposed ResAlign framework is novel, technically sound, and empirically validated.  While there are some limitations related to computational overhead and approximations, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The potential impact of ResAlign on the responsible use of diffusion models is considerable. I would expect this to influence the field significantly.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Perovskite-R1: A Domain-Specialized LLM for Intelligent Discovery of Precursor Additives and Experimental Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16307v1)
- **Authors**: Xin-De Wang, Zhi-Rui Chen, Peng-Jie Guo, Ze-Feng Gao, Cheng Mu, Zhong-Yi Lu
- **Abstract**: Perovskite solar cells (PSCs) have rapidly emerged as a leading contender in next-generation photovoltaic technologies, owing to their exceptional power conversion efficiencies and advantageous material properties. Despite these advances, challenges such as long-term stability, environmental sustainability, and scalable manufacturing continue to hinder their commercialization. Precursor additive engineering has shown promise in addressing these issues by enhancing both the performance and durability of PSCs. However, the explosive growth of scientific literature and the complex interplay of materials, processes, and device architectures make it increasingly difficult for researchers to efficiently access, organize, and utilize domain knowledge in this rapidly evolving field. To address this gap, we introduce Perovskite-R1, a specialized large language model (LLM) with advanced reasoning capabilities tailored for the discovery and design of PSC precursor additives. By systematically mining and curating 1,232 high-quality scientific publications and integrating a comprehensive library of 33,269 candidate materials, we constructed a domain-specific instruction-tuning dataset using automated question-answer generation and chain-of-thought reasoning. Fine-tuning the QwQ-32B model on this dataset resulted in Perovskite-R1, which can intelligently synthesize literature insights and generate innovative and practical solutions for defect passivation and the selection of precursor additives. Experimental validation of several model-proposed strategies confirms their effectiveness in improving material stability and performance. Our work demonstrates the potential of domain-adapted LLMs in accelerating materials discovery and provides a closed-loop framework for intelligent, data-driven advancements in perovskite photovoltaic research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Perovskite-R1, a domain-specialized large language model (LLM) designed for the intelligent discovery and design of precursor additives and experimental design in perovskite solar cells (PSCs). To construct Perovskite-R1, the authors mined and curated a dataset of 1,232 high-quality scientific publications and a library of 33,269 candidate materials. They then used automated question-answer generation and chain-of-thought reasoning to create a domain-specific instruction-tuning dataset, and fine-tuned the QwQ-32B model using this dataset. The resulting Perovskite-R1 model can synthesize literature insights, generate innovative solutions for defect passivation, and select precursor additives. Experimental validation of model-proposed strategies confirmed the effectiveness of these strategies in improving material stability and performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the domain adaptation of a large language model specifically for perovskite research, particularly for precursor additive design. While LLMs have been applied to materials science before, this paper distinguishes itself by its focus on a specific subfield and the creation of a specialized instruction-tuning dataset tailored to that domain. The application of chain-of-thought reasoning and the closed-loop experimental validation are also noteworthy. However, the base model (QwQ-32B) and the general techniques of fine-tuning and instruction tuning are not novel in themselves.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the potential to accelerate materials discovery in a complex and rapidly evolving field. By automating the process of literature review, hypothesis generation, and experimental design, Perovskite-R1 could significantly reduce the time and effort required to identify promising precursor additives. The experimental validation demonstrates the potential for real-world impact, showing that the model's suggestions can lead to improved material properties. The paper also introduces a closed-loop data-driven framework that facilitates intelligent advancements in perovskite photovoltaic research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Domain-specific focus:** Perovskite-R1's targeted design allows it to excel in a specific area of materials science, leading to more accurate and relevant results compared to general-purpose LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive dataset:** The curated dataset of scientific publications and candidate materials provides a solid foundation for training the LLM.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The experimental results validate the model's recommendations, demonstrating its practical utility.
    *   **Closed-loop framework:** The paper establishes a clear pathway from language model generation to physical experimentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Base model dependence:** The performance of Perovskite-R1 is reliant on the capabilities of the base model (QwQ-32B).
    *   **Limited scope of experimental validation:**  While promising, the experimental validation is limited to a few specific additives and materials. More extensive validation would strengthen the findings.
    *   **The experiments could be expanded**: Further validation on different perovskite materials, devices, or structures will further increase the credibility of this work.
    *   **Lack of detailed information**: Further expansion of the chain-of-thought of LLM will make this work stronger.
    *   **Scalability of approach**: Lack of discussion on the scalability of approach to new material systems is a weakness.

*   **Potential Influence:** If Perovskite-R1 proves to be a robust and generalizable tool, it could significantly impact the field of perovskite solar cell research, and serve as a template for other domain-specific LLMs in materials science. It has the potential to:
    *   Accelerate the discovery of new materials and additives
    *   Reduce the cost and time associated with materials development
    *   Promote the adoption of data-driven approaches in materials science

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution with clear novelty in its specific domain adaptation, and demonstrated significance through experimental validation. However, dependence on a base model, scope of validation experiments, and general dependence of LLM quality on training data somewhat limits the impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Re:Form -- Reducing Human Priors in Scalable Formal Software Verification with RL in LLMs: A Preliminary Study on Dafny
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16331v1)
- **Authors**: Chuanhao Yan, Fengdi Che, Xuhan Huang, Xu Xu, Xin Li, Yizhi Li, Xingwei Qu, Jingzhe Shi, Zhuangzhuang He, Chenghua Lin, Yaodong Yang, Binhang Yuan, Hang Zhao, Yu Qiao, Bowen Zhou, Jie Fu
- **Abstract**: Existing informal language-based (e.g., human language) Large Language Models (LLMs) trained with Reinforcement Learning (RL) face a significant challenge: their verification processes, which provide crucial training signals, are neither reliable nor scalable. In fact, the prevalent large proprietary models could hardly generate verifiable programs. A promising yet largely uncharted alternative is formal language-based reasoning. Grounding LLMs in rigorous formal systems where generative models operate in formal language spaces (e.g., Dafny) enables the automatic and mathematically provable verification of their reasoning processes and outcomes. This capability is pivotal for achieving large-scale, reliable formal software verification. It is a common practice to employ human-annotated chain-of-thought and other human priors to induce the reasoning and coding capabilities of LLMs. Unfortunately, it becomes unacceptably all-consuming to provide such priors for supervising complex programming tasks. In this work, we systematically explore ways to reduce human priors with the formal language, Dafny, as the main environment for our pilot study. Our pipeline mainly relies on introducing an automatic and scalable data curation pipeline, and careful RL designs integrated with feedback from the formal language verifier. We introduce DafnyComp, a benchmark of compositional formal programs with auto-formalized specifications for specification reasoning. Our supervised fine-tuning (SFT) stage enables even small models (e.g., 0.5B) to generate syntactically valid and verifiable Dafny code, surpassing proprietary models. RL with regularization further improves performance, achieving stronger generalization to out-of-domain tasks and outperforming all strong baselines on the challenging DafnyComp benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Re:Form - Reducing Human Priors in Scalable Formal Software Verification with RL in LLMs: A Preliminary Study on Dafny":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates reducing human priors in training Large Language Models (LLMs) for formal software verification. They focus on generating formal specifications for Dafny code using Reinforcement Learning (RL).  Their approach, termed "Re:Form," minimizes human intervention by using LLMs to seed training data, eliminating natural-language chain-of-thought reasoning, and relying on the Dafny verifier for automatic feedback. They introduce "DafnyComp," a new benchmark for compositional formal programs, and demonstrate that their supervised fine-tuning (SFT) stage, even with small models, can generate syntactically valid and verifiable Dafny code.  RL with regularization further improves performance, generalization, and outperforms strong baselines on DafnyComp.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The paper presents a compelling approach to reducing human dependency in the LLM training pipeline for formal verification. The idea of automatic formal code specification with human priors as minimum as possible is novel. Using automated verifiers like Dafny is a step in the right direction in a field that is often plagued with human based RLHF training.
    *   The introduction of DafnyComp benchmark is novel and addresses the need for evaluating compositionality in formal verification tasks.
    *   However, the core techniques (RL with LLMs, distillation, etc.) are not new. The novelty lies in the specific combination and application to this formal verification problem.
    *   The idea of exploiting the logical subset relation in formal languages for reward shaping (subset rewards) is a good way to prevent generating trivially satisfying proofs.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a critical challenge: scalability and reliability of LLMs in safety-critical domains where code correctness is paramount.
    *   Demonstrating that smaller models, trained with a principled reduction of human priors, can outperform large proprietary models is significant. It suggests a path towards more efficient and verifiable AI systems for code generation.
    *   The paper contributes a valuable benchmark (DafnyComp) that can spur further research in this area.
    *   The work showcases a move away from human supervision to a scalable self-improvement paradigm.
    *   The paper's focus on formal language reasoning offers a promising alternative to the limitations of informal language-based LLMs that often produce non-verifiable code.
    *   **Limitations:** The paper is preliminary and might lack some of the depth or breadth that would be expected in a more complete study. The claim of minimal human priors is always questionable as the models and RL are still human designed choices. The experimental evidence in the paper is adequate, and it is not very extensive and requires more tests.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with a focus on reducing human priors.
    *   Introduction of a relevant benchmark (DafnyComp).
    *   Demonstration of improved performance and generalization.
    *   Rigorous evaluation metrics (validation rate, verification rate, specification superiority rate).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The "minimal-prior" claim could be stronger.  Human choices still heavily influence model architecture, reward design, etc.
    *   The limited scope of the study (preliminary study) leaves room for further exploration and validation.
    *   The long term results of RL based on the Dafny language would be interesting.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in LLMs for software engineering, shifting the focus towards formal verification and reducing reliance on human data and supervision.
    *   DafnyComp could become a standard benchmark for evaluating compositional reasoning capabilities in formal verification tasks.
    *   The approach could be extended to other formal languages and verification systems.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant approach to training LLMs for formal software verification by reducing human priors. While the individual techniques are not entirely new, their combination and application to this problem are innovative. The development of DafnyComp contributes a valuable resource to the field. The experimental results are promising, demonstrating improved performance and generalization capabilities compared to existing methods and proprietary models.
However, the "preliminary" nature of the study and the inherent limitations in completely eliminating human priors are weaknesses. Also, there appears to be no attempt at abalation study of the human priors, to make the paper stronger. The reliance on the Dafny verifier as the "ground truth" introduces a certain degree of bias, and long term exploration into this language is required. Given the novelty, significance, clear methodology, and promising results, it is assigned a solid 7.5 for impact and future potential.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Navigating Large-Pose Challenge for High-Fidelity Face Reenactment with Video Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16341v1)
- **Authors**: Mingtao Guo, Guanyu Xing, Yanci Zhang, Yanli Liu
- **Abstract**: Face reenactment aims to generate realistic talking head videos by transferring motion from a driving video to a static source image while preserving the source identity. Although existing methods based on either implicit or explicit keypoints have shown promise, they struggle with large pose variations due to warping artifacts or the limitations of coarse facial landmarks. In this paper, we present the Face Reenactment Video Diffusion model (FRVD), a novel framework for high-fidelity face reenactment under large pose changes. Our method first employs a motion extractor to extract implicit facial keypoints from the source and driving images to represent fine-grained motion and to perform motion alignment through a warping module. To address the degradation introduced by warping, we introduce a Warping Feature Mapper (WFM) that maps the warped source image into the motion-aware latent space of a pretrained image-to-video (I2V) model. This latent space encodes rich priors of facial dynamics learned from large-scale video data, enabling effective warping correction and enhancing temporal coherence. Extensive experiments show that FRVD achieves superior performance over existing methods in terms of pose accuracy, identity preservation, and visual quality, especially in challenging scenarios with extreme pose variations.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper, "Navigating Large-Pose Challenge for High-Fidelity Face Reenactment with Video Diffusion Model." Here's a summary and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces FRVD, a novel Face Reenactment Video Diffusion model, designed to improve the fidelity of face reenactment, especially when dealing with large pose variations.  The core idea involves extracting implicit facial keypoints from both the source and driving videos using a "Motion Extractor" and warping the source image based on these keypoints. To address the warping artifacts, a "Warping Feature Mapper (WFM)" maps the warped features into the motion-aware latent space of a pre-trained image-to-video (I2V) model (specifically Stable Video Diffusion).  This leverages the I2V model's learned priors on facial dynamics to reconstruct degraded regions and enhance temporal coherence. The approach also uses a rectified guidance signal during training to improve source image appearance. The authors demonstrate that FRVD outperforms existing methods in pose accuracy, identity preservation, and visual quality, especially with extreme poses. It also includes a "Motion Alignment Module" to support cross-identity reenactment during inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing techniques (implicit keypoints, warping, and diffusion models) but presents a specific architecture and training strategy that appears to address a critical weakness in existing face reenactment methods – robustness to large pose variations.  The *integration of a motion-aware latent space* from a pre-trained I2V model for warping correction is a key novel aspect. Instead of relying on pixel-level inpainting, which struggles with limited source image information in severely warped regions, leveraging the learned priors of an I2V model seems a viable and clever approach. The Warping Feature Mapper seems to be the key ingredient that effectively uses the I2V's ability to generate natural dynamics. While the Motion Extractor is not entirely novel, its inclusion and interaction with the WFM in the proposed pipeline contributes to the final output quality. Also, the Motion Alignment Module during inference seems to be a relatively simple, yet crucial addition for cross-identity reenactment.

*   **Significance:** Face reenactment is a relevant and active research area. The method seems to solve a key issue of generating realistic videos with substantial pose changes. High-fidelity results under large pose variations are quite important to several important applications like creating realistic virtual avatars, improved video conferencing, and character role-playing in games. In this regard, the improvements shown in the qualitative results and the metrics are clearly interesting. The qualitative comparisons show improved visual fidelity over existing techniques, especially in large-pose scenarios. The method consistently demonstrates superior performance compared to other state-of-the-art approaches. The extensive quantitative results, including the user study (which focuses on perceptual metrics, which are quite relevant for face reenactment) also strengthens the claims of the authors.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The idea of incorporating the motion-aware latent space of a pre-trained I2V model for warping correction is interesting and potentially a key differentiator.
    *   The paper addresses a relevant limitation in existing face reenactment methods.
    *   The experimental results (both quantitative and qualitative) are compelling and demonstrate superior performance.
    *   The paper is well-written and well-organized.
    *   The ablation studies provide evidence of the effectiveness of the WFM and rectified guidance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a pre-trained I2V model (Stable Video Diffusion) means that the performance is tied to the capabilities of that underlying model. Future improvements to video diffusion models may directly benefit FRVD. Therefore, the gains compared to competing approaches may potentially diminish over time.
    *   The paper could provide more detailed analysis of the Motion Extractor's performance. While its primary aim is pose estimation and motion alignment, an evaluation of its accuracy would improve the reader's understanding of its contributions to the framework.
    *   The paper mentions the inference speed (4 minutes per 100 frames) which can be considered slow for real-time applications. This is a considerable limitation, and it should be a significant focus of future research in this direction. While the authors mention plans to address this, they do not present any solutions in this direction.
    *   The reliance on large-scale datasets (VFHQ) is a potential limitation, in terms of reproducibility and potential biases in the data.
    * The reliance on cloud APIs (like MediaPipe, ArcFace) also creates a concern about reproducibility and accessibility of the code.

* **Potential Impact:**

    * The FRVD model can be used to generate high-quality training data for downstream tasks, such as video editing and special effects.
    * The approach could inspire new research directions in face reenactment, exploring the integration of learned priors from other types of generative models.
    * The method is useful for creating digital avatars, personalized content, and visual effects in various applications such as video games, film production, and social media.
    * The approach offers a means of enhancing visual communications for users with disabilities, such as facial paralysis or motor impairments.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel integration of existing techniques to address a significant challenge in face reenactment. The experimental results provide strong evidence that FRVD outperforms existing methods. However, the reliance on pre-trained models, the inference speed limitation, and the dependency on external APIs are also notable weaknesses. Taking these points into consideration, the method is not an earth-shattering paradigm shift but rather an iterative improvement that builds upon existing ideas. Thus, it does not achieve the highest mark.

**Score: 7.5**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Depth Gives a False Sense of Privacy: LLM Internal States Inversion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16372v1)
- **Authors**: Tian Dong, Yan Meng, Shaofeng Li, Guoxing Chen, Zhen Liu, Haojin Zhu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated into daily routines, yet they raise significant privacy and safety concerns. Recent research proposes collaborative inference, which outsources the early-layer inference to ensure data locality, and introduces model safety auditing based on inner neuron patterns. Both techniques expose the LLM's Internal States (ISs), which are traditionally considered irreversible to inputs due to optimization challenges and the highly abstract representations in deep layers. In this work, we challenge this assumption by proposing four inversion attacks that significantly improve the semantic similarity and token matching rate of inverted inputs. Specifically, we first develop two white-box optimization-based attacks tailored for low-depth and high-depth ISs. These attacks avoid local minima convergence, a limitation observed in prior work, through a two-phase inversion process. Then, we extend our optimization attack under more practical black-box weight access by leveraging the transferability between the source and the derived LLMs. Additionally, we introduce a generation-based attack that treats inversion as a translation task, employing an inversion model to reconstruct inputs. Extensive evaluation of short and long prompts from medical consulting and coding assistance datasets and 6 LLMs validates the effectiveness of our inversion attacks. Notably, a 4,112-token long medical consulting prompt can be nearly perfectly inverted with 86.88 F1 token matching from the middle layer of Llama-3 model. Finally, we evaluate four practical defenses that we found cannot perfectly prevent ISs inversion and draw conclusions for future mitigation design.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Depth Gives a False Sense of Privacy: LLM Internal States Inversion" investigates the feasibility of inverting internal states (ISs) of Large Language Models (LLMs) to recover user inputs (prompts).  The authors challenge the assumption that deep-layer ISs offer inherent privacy due to their abstract representations and optimization challenges. They propose four novel inversion attacks: two white-box optimization-based attacks (Embedding Recovery (ER) and Token Basis Selection (TBS)) tailored for low-depth and high-depth ISs respectively, a black-box optimization-based attack leveraging transferability between LLMs, and a generation-based attack that treats inversion as a translation task. The effectiveness of these attacks is demonstrated through extensive evaluations on short and long prompts from medical consulting and coding assistance datasets across six LLMs. They further evaluate the effectiveness of defenses and find them lacking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in systematically exploring the input inversion risk associated with LLM internal states (ISs).  While embedding inversion has been studied for smaller language models, the authors are the first to tackle the specific challenges posed by the deeper architectures, larger vocabulary sizes, and inference-oriented representations within modern LLMs. Their two-phase optimization strategy (ER and TBS), projection module, and black-box transfer attack demonstrate significant adaptations to overcome limitations of previous inversion techniques. The generation-based attack is not entirely novel, but the adaptation to LLM ISs using encoder-decoder models with novel architectural components is a good contribution.

**Significance:** The paper has significant implications for the privacy and security of LLM-based systems. It directly undermines the perceived safety of collaborative inference and model safety auditing techniques that rely on exposing LLM ISs. By demonstrating successful prompt recovery from ISs, the work highlights the need for more robust privacy measures. The findings are relevant to scenarios where ISs are exposed to third parties such as honest-but-curious inference servers and third-party auditors. Moreover, it has implications beyond the specific inversion task, raising broader concerns about the information leakage potential of internal representations in deep learning models. The analysis of existing defenses, and their inadequacy, will be helpful for researchers focusing on mitigation techniques.

**Strengths:**
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct thorough experiments on multiple LLMs, datasets, and prompt lengths, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of their proposed attacks.
*   **Well-Defined Attacks:** The attacks are presented clearly, with detailed algorithms and explanations of their underlying rationale. The motivation for ER and TBS is well-articulated, clearly demonstrating the problems with directly adapting previous approaches to LLM IS inversion.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The study directly addresses real-world scenarios like collaborative inference and safety auditing, making the results highly relevant to practitioners.
*   **Strong Problem Framing:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing inversion techniques and frames the specific challenges in the context of LLMs very well.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Black-box attack limitations:** The Black-box attack's performance is limited by the need for a surrogate model and by distributional differences in the adversary data. A more robust black-box attack that can operate effectively without a closely aligned surrogate model or training data would be a stronger contribution.
*   **Limited Defense Evaluation:** While the defense evaluation highlights the inadequacy of existing methods, it does not explore more sophisticated, targeted defenses that might be tailored to counteract the specific vulnerabilities exposed by the proposed attacks.
*   **High Computational Cost:** The optimization-based attacks can be computationally intensive, especially for larger models and longer prompts.  This may limit their practicality in some real-world scenarios.

**Influence:**
The paper will likely influence future research in several areas:
*   **Privacy-Preserving LLM Techniques:** It will spur the development of more robust privacy-preserving techniques for collaborative inference, model safety auditing, and representation engineering.
*   **Inversion Attack Development:** It will motivate the development of more sophisticated and efficient inversion attacks that can overcome the limitations of the presented methods (e.g., better black-box attacks).
*   **Understanding Deep Representations:** It will contribute to a deeper understanding of the information content and leakage potential of internal representations in deep learning models.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution to the field by demonstrating the feasibility of inverting LLM internal states, highlighting a significant privacy vulnerability. The proposed attacks are novel, well-defined, and supported by extensive empirical evidence. The paper has practical implications for collaborative inference and model auditing techniques and will likely influence future research in privacy-preserving LLMs and deep learning. While the black-box attack has limitations and the defense evaluation could be more extensive, the strengths of the paper outweigh its weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Application of LLM Guided Reinforcement Learning in Formation Control with Collision Avoidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16382v1)
- **Authors**: Chenhao Yao, Zike Yuan, Xiaoxu Liu, Chi Zhu
- **Abstract**: Multi-Agent Systems (MAS) excel at accomplishing complex objectives through the collaborative efforts of individual agents. Among the methodologies employed in MAS, Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) stands out as one of the most efficacious algorithms. However, when confronted with the complex objective of Formation Control with Collision Avoidance (FCCA): designing an effective reward function that facilitates swift convergence of the policy network to an optimal solution. In this paper, we introduce a novel framework that aims to overcome this challenge. By giving large language models (LLMs) on the prioritization of tasks and the observable information available to each agent, our framework generates reward functions that can be dynamically adjusted online based on evaluation outcomes by employing more advanced evaluation metrics rather than the rewards themselves. This mechanism enables the MAS to simultaneously achieve formation control and obstacle avoidance in dynamic environments with enhanced efficiency, requiring fewer iterations to reach superior performance levels. Our empirical studies, conducted in both simulation and real-world settings, validate the practicality and effectiveness of our proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for multi-agent formation control with collision avoidance (FCCA) using Large Language Model (LLM) guided reinforcement learning (RL). The core idea is to leverage LLMs to generate and dynamically refine reward functions based on high-level evaluation metrics, moving beyond simple reward magnitude feedback.  The system provides the LLM with agent observations, allowing the LLM to create initial reward functions.  These functions are then evaluated based on predefined performance criteria, and feedback is provided to the LLM for online adjustments, leading to improved performance with fewer iterations. The approach is validated through simulations and real-world experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The application of LLMs to *dynamically* generate and refine reward functions in MARL, particularly for the FCCA problem, is a significant contribution. While LLMs have been used to generate initial reward functions, the online adaptive tuning based on high-level metrics (beyond simple reward magnitude) distinguishes this work. The integration of LLMs for this task, moving away from hand-crafted reward functions, presents a potentially powerful paradigm shift.  The combination of agent observation feedback to the LLM and the subsequent reward function refinement is also a novel aspect.

**Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to automate and improve the reward function design process, which is often a bottleneck in MARL.  Complex tasks, like FCCA, require careful balancing of multiple objectives.  The proposed method offers a means to achieve this balance more efficiently, potentially reducing the time and effort required to train high-performing multi-agent systems. The sim-to-real validation strengthens the practical relevance of the approach. The increased efficiency from the dynamic update of the reward function, potentially saving a significant amount of compute resources compared to retraining, provides more value to the method.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of reward function design in complex MARL tasks.
*   **Novel integration of LLMs:** The application of LLMs for dynamic reward function refinement is a key strength.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes both simulation and real-world experiments to validate the approach. The comparison with a human-designed method provides a concrete benchmark. The LLM-based approach showed higher success rate, lower time cost and lower formation error.
*   **Well-defined methodology:** The approach is explained with reasonable clarity and the algorithms are outlined effectively.
*   **Demonstrated improvement:** The reward functions can be trained more efficiently with the help of LLM.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM limitations acknowledged but not fully addressed:**  The paper acknowledges that LLM may lose track of initial requirements. Further research is needed to address this, possibly through memory mechanisms or better prompt engineering strategies.
*   **Limited scope of experiments:** The real-world experiments, while important, are conducted in a relatively controlled environment. More complex, unstructured environments could provide a more rigorous test.
*   **Dependence on evaluation metrics:** The quality of the high-level evaluation metrics is crucial to the success of the approach. The paper doesn't delve deeply into how these metrics are selected and refined. What happens if the initial performance criteria are inappropriate?
*   **Scalability concerns:** While the paper mentions the multi-agent aspect, the scalability of the LLM-based reward function generation to a large number of agents isn't addressed in detail.
*   **Lack of robustness details:** How does the performance of the LLM-based model degrades when certain observations are not accessible? Does the LLM get stuck in local optima?
*   **Few baselines:** It compares the approach to human-designed approaches and ORCA-F. Other MARL algorithms could be compared in the experiments.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper could influence the direction of MARL research by encouraging further exploration of LLMs for reward function design and adaptation. The dynamic refinement approach is particularly promising and could be extended to other complex multi-agent tasks. The integration of high-level evaluation metrics provides a valuable framework for evaluating and improving the performance of RL agents.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of MARL by successfully integrating LLMs for dynamic reward function refinement. The combination of agent observation data and LLMs to make complex decisions adds value to the area of robotics research. The clear problem definition, comprehensive evaluation, and well-defined methodology are strengths of the work. However, the limitations related to LLM consistency, scalability, reliance on evaluation metrics, and limited scope of experiments prevent it from achieving a higher score. Addressing these weaknesses in future work would further strengthen the approach and increase its impact on the field. It should be noted that the LLM contribution is mainly related to the efficiency of the existing RL, and less related to the RL performance itself. The experiments show the speed of reward adjustment is increased through the methods proposed in this paper.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Knowledge-aware Diffusion-Enhanced Multimedia Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16396v1)
- **Authors**: Xian Mo, Fei Liu, Rui Tang, Jintao, Gao, Hao Liu
- **Abstract**: Multimedia recommendations aim to use rich multimedia content to enhance historical user-item interaction information, which can not only indicate the content relatedness among items but also reveal finer-grained preferences of users. In this paper, we propose a Knowledge-aware Diffusion-Enhanced architecture using contrastive learning paradigms (KDiffE) for multimedia recommendations. Specifically, we first utilize original user-item graphs to build an attention-aware matrix into graph neural networks, which can learn the importance between users and items for main view construction. The attention-aware matrix is constructed by adopting a random walk with a restart strategy, which can preserve the importance between users and items to generate aggregation of attention-aware node features. Then, we propose a guided diffusion model to generate strongly task-relevant knowledge graphs with less noise for constructing a knowledge-aware contrastive view, which utilizes user embeddings with an edge connected to an item to guide the generation of strongly task-relevant knowledge graphs for enhancing the item's semantic information. We perform comprehensive experiments on three multimedia datasets that reveal the effectiveness of our KDiffE and its components on various state-of-the-art methods. Our source codes are available https://github.com/1453216158/KDiffE.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Knowledge-aware Diffusion-Enhanced Multimedia Recommendation."

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel architecture called Knowledge-aware Diffusion-Enhanced (KDiffE) for multimedia recommendation. The core idea is to leverage a diffusion model to generate a task-relevant knowledge graph with reduced noise, and an attention-aware mechanism for better user-item interaction modelling. The KDiffE model first constructs an attention-aware matrix incorporated into a graph neural network (GNN) to capture the importance between users and items. Subsequently, a guided diffusion model generates a knowledge-aware contrastive view, enhancing item semantic information by incorporating user embeddings. Contrastive learning is then applied to enhance representations, and experiments on multimedia datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the method and its individual components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty in approach:** The combination of diffusion models with attention-aware GNNs for multimedia recommendation appears relatively novel. Using user embeddings to guide diffusion is a potentially valuable contribution to robust knowledge graph generation.
    *   **Attention-aware Mechanism:** The proposed attention-aware matrix construction can capture the importance between users and items to enhance user-item interactions.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper includes detailed experiments and ablation studies that contribute to a thorough understanding of the different components within the KDiffE model. Experiments across three multimedia datasets further strengthen the validity of the claims.
    *   **Addressing sparsity:** The paper seeks to address sparsity in multimedia content, a crucial problem for real-world application, and provides a potential avenue by building contrastive views using user embeddings.
    *   **Interpretability:** The attention-aware S provides enhanced interpretability, as the Jaccard index reveals the similarity between user and item interactions and can be easily observed.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited novelty in individual components:** Diffusion models and attention mechanisms are independently well-studied within recommendation systems and deep learning. The primary novelty lies in their specific combination, but the individual contributions may not be groundbreaking. It needs to be examined if the improvements are just a result of sophisticated hyperparameter tuning and model architecture rather than real novelty.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture appears complex, involving multiple stages and components (attention-aware GNN, diffusion model, contrastive learning). The increased model complexity needs to be carefully weighed against gains in performance.
    *   **Computational cost:** Generating the diffusion model is a heavy task. The complexity grows in time, which can be detrimental.
    *   **Dependence on pre-trained models:** The approach relies on pre-trained deep networks for feature extraction, which could limit the model's adaptability to new domains. The quality of these pre-trained models also influences the final performance.
    *   **Limited novelty in the loss function:** The overall model training involves a contrastive loss, which is often adapted from existing work.
    *   **Runtime complexity:** The paper does not address the high time complexity problem.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a significant problem in multimedia recommendation: effectively leveraging multimodal information while mitigating sparsity.
    *   The combination of techniques has the potential to improve the robustness and accuracy of recommendations.
    *   The experiments and analysis provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of each component, and it can serve as a benchmark for future research.
    *   The paper can lead to more effective and robust multimedia recommendation systems, with potential applications in e-commerce, content platforms, and other domains.

*   **Justification for the Score:**

    The paper presents a relatively novel combination of existing techniques to solve a significant problem in the multimedia recommendation space. While the individual components may not be groundbreaking, the specific architecture and the incorporation of user embeddings to guide the diffusion process are noteworthy. The thorough experimental evaluation contributes to the validity of the approach. However, the model's complexity, computational cost, and reliance on pre-trained models limit the broader practical applicability, and it is unclear how unique the architecture can be given previous works in graph-based multimedia recommendation. Given these considerations, the novelty isn't fully transformative.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Code LLM Robustness to Prompt Perturbations via Layer-Aware Model Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16407v1)
- **Authors**: Shuhan Liu, Xing Hu, Kerui Huang, Xiaohu Yang, David Lo, Xin Xia
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in code generation, where the natural language prompt plays a crucial role in conveying user intent to the model. However, prior studies have shown that LLMs are highly sensitive to prompt perturbations. Minor modifications in wording, syntax, or formatting can significantly reduce the functional correctness of generated code. As perturbations frequently occur in real-world scenarios, improving the robustness of LLMs to prompt perturbations is essential for ensuring reliable performance in practical code generation. In this paper, we introduce CREME (Code Robustness Enhancement via Model Editing), a novel approach that enhances LLM robustness through targeted parameter updates. CREME first identifies robustness-sensitive layers by comparing hidden states between an original prompt and its perturbed variant. Then, it performs lightweight parameter editing at the identified layer to reduce performance degradation. We evaluate CREME on two widely used code generation benchmarks (HumanEval and MBPP) along with their perturbed counterparts. Experimental results show that CREME improves Pass@1 accuracy by 63% on perturbed prompts while maintaining stable performance on clean inputs, with accuracy deviations within 1%. Further analysis reveals that robustness-sensitive layers are primarily concentrated in the middle and deeper layers of the network, and their locations vary across different model architectures. These insights provide a valuable foundation for developing future robustness-oriented editing strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CREME (CodeLLM Robustness Enhancement via Model Editing), a novel framework designed to improve the robustness of large language models (LLMs) for code generation against natural language prompt perturbations. CREME identifies robustness-sensitive layers within the LLM by comparing hidden states between original and perturbed prompts. It then performs lightweight parameter editing at these identified layers to align representations and reduce performance degradation on perturbed inputs, while preserving performance on clean inputs.  The method is evaluated on HumanEval and MBPP datasets, demonstrating significant improvements in Pass@1 accuracy on perturbed prompts with minimal impact on clean prompt accuracy. The paper also provides an analysis of the location of robustness-sensitive layers and introduces a new evaluation metric (G-RIR) to quantify the generalization ability of robustness enhancement methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a novel approach to improving LLM robustness that combines causal tracing for layer identification with localized parameter editing. This is a relatively new area, as traditional methods often rely on input modifications or architectural augmentations. The idea of using knowledge editing techniques, previously applied to factual knowledge, for prompt robustness is a worthwhile exploration. The introduction of the G-RIR metric is a small but valuable contribution to the evaluation methodology in this domain. The empirical analysis of robustness-sensitive layer locations across different models and perturbation types is also novel and insightful.

**Significance:**

LLMs are increasingly used in code generation, and their sensitivity to prompt variations poses a significant challenge to their reliability. CREME offers a lightweight and potentially more efficient way to address this issue compared to full retraining or architectural modifications. The improved robustness could lead to more reliable code generation in real-world scenarios where prompts are often imperfect.  The identified clustering of robustness-sensitive layers suggests that there may be opportunities to develop more targeted and efficient robustness-enhancing interventions.  The consistent improvement on perturbed prompts across multiple benchmarks (MBPP, HumanEval) increases the reliability of the method. It's also significant that the model can improve upon robustness while retaining, or even improving slightly, the clean-prompt performance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of prompt sensitivity in code LLMs.
*   **Novel Approach:** CREME is a novel and well-motivated approach that leverages existing techniques in a new and relevant way.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation with multiple datasets, perturbation types, and comparison with strong baselines.
*   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of robustness-sensitive layer locations and the introduction of G-RIR provide valuable insights.
*   **Well-Written:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Model and Dataset Diversity:** The evaluation is limited to two open-source code LLMs and two code generation datasets. Evaluating on more diverse models and datasets, including proprietary models, would strengthen the findings.
*   **Perturbation Scope:** While the paper examines a range of natural language variations, it might benefit from including robustness against more *adversarial* prompt perturbations or variations arising from other languages.
*   **Scalability:** While described as lightweight, the causal tracing process could be computationally expensive for very large models.
*   **Generalization of the Edit:** The approach still edits *one prompt* per category to then generalize; exploring if a broader range of prompts for edits is required for improved general robustness would be useful.
*   **Practicality in Large-Scale Systems:** The application of CREME in very large production systems, where rapid model iterations and deployments are common, should be discussed.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM robustness by introducing a novel and effective framework. While there are some limitations in the scope of the evaluation and potential scalability concerns, the core ideas are well-developed and the experimental results are compelling. The paper clearly tackles a relevant and challenging problem and offers a promising solution. The insights regarding layer distribution are particularly valuable for future research. However, the limited model diversity and need for empirical analysis makes it difficult to assign a much higher score than what is presented below.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### GG-BBQ: German Gender Bias Benchmark for Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16410v1)
- **Authors**: Shalaka Satheesh, Katrin Klug, Katharina Beckh, Héctor Allende-Cid, Sebastian Houben, Teena Hassan
- **Abstract**: Within the context of Natural Language Processing (NLP), fairness evaluation is often associated with the assessment of bias and reduction of associated harm. In this regard, the evaluation is usually carried out by using a benchmark dataset, for a task such as Question Answering, created for the measurement of bias in the model's predictions along various dimensions, including gender identity. In our work, we evaluate gender bias in German Large Language Models (LLMs) using the Bias Benchmark for Question Answering by Parrish et al. (2022) as a reference. Specifically, the templates in the gender identity subset of this English dataset were machine translated into German. The errors in the machine translated templates were then manually reviewed and corrected with the help of a language expert. We find that manual revision of the translation is crucial when creating datasets for gender bias evaluation because of the limitations of machine translation from English to a language such as German with grammatical gender. Our final dataset is comprised of two subsets: Subset-I, which consists of group terms related to gender identity, and Subset-II, where group terms are replaced with proper names. We evaluate several LLMs used for German NLP on this newly created dataset and report the accuracy and bias scores. The results show that all models exhibit bias, both along and against existing social stereotypes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GG-BBQ: German Gender Bias Benchmark for Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GG-BBQ, a German language benchmark dataset for evaluating gender bias in question answering (QA) systems.  The dataset is based on a translation and adaptation of the gender identity subset of the English BBQ dataset. The authors emphasize the crucial role of manual review and correction during translation due to the grammatical gender differences between English and German. The final dataset comprises two subsets: one using group terms (e.g., "man," "woman") and another using proper names.  The paper then presents an evaluation of several German LLMs using this new dataset, reporting accuracy and bias scores.  The results indicate that the evaluated models exhibit gender bias, both aligning with and contradicting existing social stereotypes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in creating a *German-language* benchmark for gender bias in QA. While BBQ exists in English, the lack of a direct equivalent for German-speaking contexts necessitates such a translation and adaptation. However, *the methodological novelty is limited*.  The paper relies on existing approaches for bias evaluation (Parrish et al., Jin et al.) and simply applies them to the translated dataset. The use of machine translation followed by manual correction is a standard approach to dataset creation and doesn't represent a significant innovation in itself. Also, the reliance on the methodology used in the English version might make the final dataset incapable of capturing some cultural differences between countries.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant in several respects:

    *   **Resource Creation:** It provides a valuable resource (GG-BBQ) for the German NLP community. This dataset can facilitate further research into bias detection and mitigation in German LLMs.
    *   **Empirical Findings:** The evaluation of existing models highlights the presence of gender bias in German LLMs, underscoring the importance of addressing this issue.  This has implications for the fair and equitable deployment of these models in real-world applications.
    *   **Methodological Contribution:**  The paper underscores the challenges of translating bias benchmarks, especially between languages with different grammatical structures (gendered vs. non-gendered). The emphasis on manual review is a valuable lesson for future dataset creation efforts.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the motivation for creating a German-specific bias benchmark.
    *   The methodology is sound and well-explained.
    *   The empirical results are clearly presented and analyzed.
    *   The paper acknowledges limitations, such as potential cultural biases not captured during translation.
    *   The explicit acknowledgement that translation can be error prone in regard to the final evaluation is well highlighted.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The methodological approach is largely derivative of existing work.
    *   The study focuses solely on gender identity, while intersectional biases (e.g., gender and race) are not addressed.
    *   The paper does not explore mitigation strategies for the identified biases, focusing only on evaluation. This makes the contribution not holistic, and the paper seems less relevant in regards to the final goal of making less biased models.
    *   The reliance on a single language expert could introduce annotator bias.
    *   The lack of non-binary terms in German presents a limitation.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The GG-BBQ dataset has the potential to influence research in German NLP by providing a standardized tool for evaluating and comparing bias mitigation techniques. The findings could also inform the development of more fair and equitable German LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a tangible contribution by providing a needed German-language bias benchmark. However, the lack of methodological novelty, the narrow focus on gender identity, and the absence of bias mitigation strategies limit its overall impact. The paper is well-executed and provides valuable empirical results, but it does not represent a groundbreaking advancement in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Identifying Pre-training Data in LLMs: A Neuron Activation-Based Detection Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16414v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyi Tang, Zhihao Zhu, Yi Yang
- **Abstract**: The performance of large language models (LLMs) is closely tied to their training data, which can include copyrighted material or private information, raising legal and ethical concerns. Additionally, LLMs face criticism for dataset contamination and internalizing biases. To address these issues, the Pre-Training Data Detection (PDD) task was proposed to identify if specific data was included in an LLM's pre-training corpus. However, existing PDD methods often rely on superficial features like prediction confidence and loss, resulting in mediocre performance. To improve this, we introduce NA-PDD, a novel algorithm analyzing differential neuron activation patterns between training and non-training data in LLMs. This is based on the observation that these data types activate different neurons during LLM inference. We also introduce CCNewsPDD, a temporally unbiased benchmark employing rigorous data transformations to ensure consistent time distributions between training and non-training data. Our experiments demonstrate that NA-PDD significantly outperforms existing methods across three benchmarks and multiple LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Identifying Pre-training Data in LLMs: A Neuron Activation-Based Detection Framework":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NA-PDD, a novel algorithm for pre-training data detection (PDD) in Large Language Models (LLMs).  Unlike existing methods that rely on superficial features like prediction confidence or loss, NA-PDD analyzes the differential neuron activation patterns between training and non-training data. The method is based on the observation that different data types activate distinct neurons during LLM inference.  The paper also introduces CCNewsPDD, a temporally unbiased benchmark designed to avoid time-drift issues present in other PDD datasets. The authors demonstrate through experiments that NA-PDD significantly outperforms existing methods across several benchmarks and LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty of this paper lies in its use of neuron activation patterns to detect pre-training data. Shifting the focus from surface-level features like loss and probabilities to internal model states (neuron activations) is a significant departure from previous approaches. While membership inference has been applied to LLMs before, using neuron activations in the way the authors do is a non-trivial contribution. The introduction of a temporally unbiased benchmark, CCNewsPDD, is also a valuable addition as it addresses a significant limitation in existing datasets. The careful design of data transformation methods (back-translation, masking, prompting) is also innovative.

**Significance:** The problem of detecting pre-training data is becoming increasingly important due to legal and ethical concerns surrounding copyright infringement, privacy violations, and the potential for bias amplification. NA-PDD offers a promising avenue for addressing these concerns, especially in scenarios where access to model internals is permitted (e.g., open-source models or internal audits). The observed improvements over existing methods suggest that neuron activation patterns can provide a more reliable signal for detecting pre-training data than surface-level features. The CCNewsPDD benchmark helps provide a more faithful evaluation of PDD methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The use of neuron activation patterns is a fresh and potentially more robust approach to pre-training data detection.
*   **Benchmark:** The CCNewsPDD benchmark addresses a key limitation of existing datasets, ensuring more accurate evaluation.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate that NA-PDD significantly outperforms existing methods on multiple benchmarks and LLMs, indicating the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Rigorous Experimental Design:** The ablation studies and sensitivity analyses are well-executed and provide valuable insights into the behavior of NA-PDD.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Applicability:** The biggest limitation is the requirement for white-box access to the LLM's internal states (neuron activations). This limits the applicability of NA-PDD to open-source models or situations where internal access is permitted, excluding black-box scenarios.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** While the paper demonstrates promising results on LLMs with up to 13 billion parameters, it is unclear how well NA-PDD would scale to larger models with hundreds of billions or trillions of parameters. Computational resource limitations prevented evaluation on larger LLMs.
*   **Reference Corpus Dependence:** The method relies on a reference corpus to label "member" and "non-member" neurons, which might not always be available or representative. The size and quality of this reference corpus can influence the accuracy of the method.

**Potential Influence:**

If the white-box requirement can be mitigated or if access to model internals becomes more common, NA-PDD could become a valuable tool for auditing LLMs and ensuring compliance with copyright and privacy regulations. The method's potential for identifying training data could also be useful for studying the memorization capabilities of LLMs and understanding how they learn from data. The CCNewsPDD benchmark is likely to become a standard dataset for evaluating PDD methods in the future.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel and effective approach to pre-training data detection, backed by strong empirical results and a well-designed benchmark. However, the significant limitation of requiring white-box access restricts its immediate applicability. While the results are promising, further research is needed to address scalability concerns and potentially reduce reliance on the reference corpus to make it more practical. The overall contribution is substantial, warranting a score of 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robust Noisy Pseudo-label Learning for Semi-supervised Medical Image Segmentation Using Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16429v1)
- **Authors**: Lin Xi, Yingliang Ma, Cheng Wang, Sandra Howell, Aldo Rinaldi, Kawal S. Rhode
- **Abstract**: Obtaining pixel-level annotations in the medical domain is both expensive and time-consuming, often requiring close collaboration between clinical experts and developers. Semi-supervised medical image segmentation aims to leverage limited annotated data alongside abundant unlabeled data to achieve accurate segmentation. However, existing semi-supervised methods often struggle to structure semantic distributions in the latent space due to noise introduced by pseudo-labels. In this paper, we propose a novel diffusion-based framework for semi-supervised medical image segmentation. Our method introduces a constraint into the latent structure of semantic labels during the denoising diffusion process by enforcing prototype-based contrastive consistency. Rather than explicitly delineating semantic boundaries, the model leverages class prototypes centralized semantic representations in the latent space as anchors. This strategy improves the robustness of dense predictions, particularly in the presence of noisy pseudo-labels. We also introduce a new publicly available benchmark: Multi-Object Segmentation in X-ray Angiography Videos (MOSXAV), which provides detailed, manually annotated segmentation ground truth for multiple anatomical structures in X-ray angiography videos. Extensive experiments on the EndoScapes2023 and MOSXAV datasets demonstrate that our method outperforms state-of-the-art medical image segmentation approaches under the semi-supervised learning setting. This work presents a robust and data-efficient diffusion model that offers enhanced flexibility and strong potential for a wide range of clinical applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel diffusion-based framework for semi-supervised medical image segmentation. It addresses the challenge of noisy pseudo-labels in semi-supervised learning by introducing a prototype-anchored contrastive learning mechanism to structure the latent space of semantic labels during the denoising diffusion process.  The method enforces prototype-based contrastive consistency, using class prototypes as anchors in the latent space to improve the robustness of predictions, especially in the presence of noisy labels.  The authors also introduce a new dataset, MOSXAV, for multi-object segmentation in X-ray angiography videos. The paper demonstrates superior performance compared to existing semi-supervised medical image segmentation methods on the EndoScapes2023 and MOSXAV datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating prototype-anchored contrastive learning within a diffusion model for semi-supervised segmentation is a good contribution. This appears to be a relatively novel approach to address the challenges of noisy pseudo-labels. Structuring the latent space during the diffusion process using prototypes is a well-motivated and potentially effective strategy.
*   **Significance:**  Semi-supervised learning is crucial in medical image analysis due to the scarcity of labeled data.  The proposed method tackles a key problem in semi-supervised learning (noisy labels) and offers a potential solution.  Improving the robustness of segmentation models to noisy labels has significant implications for real-world applications.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results on two datasets (EndoScapes2023 and the newly introduced MOSXAV) demonstrate that the method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches. The ablation studies provide further insights into the effectiveness of individual components of the proposed framework. The introduction of a new angiography dataset (MOSXAV) is a valuable contribution to the community.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and explains the proposed method clearly.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Advancement:** While the idea of using prototype-anchored contrastive learning within a diffusion model is novel, the individual components (diffusion models, contrastive learning, prototype learning) are well-established. The paper needs to highlight the specific modifications/contributions made to each of these components and emphasize the interplay between them in more detail. The paper could also spend more time discussing how the prototype method is better, and how it is different from other prototype-based methods.
*   **Dataset Specificity:** The improvements are shown primarily on datasets relating to endoscopic and X-ray data. How generalizable is this method for data outside of this domain? Is this method particularly useful for medical images with small objects/regions?
*   **Implementation Details:** Some implementation details could be clearer, such as the specific architecture of the diffusion decoder and the process of generating pseudo-labels. The sensitivity of the method to the choice of hyperparameters (e.g., the temperature parameter in the contrastive loss) should be discussed.
*   **Limited analysis of MOSXAV dataset:** While the introduction of the MOSXAV dataset is valuable, more in-depth analysis of the dataset's characteristics, challenges, and potential applications would be beneficial.

**Significance Score Justification:**

The paper presents a moderately novel approach to a relevant problem in medical image analysis (semi-supervised segmentation). The experimental results demonstrate tangible improvements over existing methods. The major weakness is that this seems to be an incremental combination of existing methods, rather than developing any fundamentally new concepts or techniques. The introduction of the MOSXAV dataset adds to the paper's value.
